- Stop median-filling an ECGDex interval that straddles a dropout — a 74-second absence was being written into the NN series as a plausible ~1 s heartbeat (5 of 5 on a real H10 night) and flagged `corrected` as though the beat had merely been mis-measured; the honest elapsed time now survives, marked `spansGap` and excluded from rMSSD, pNN50, meanRR and SDNN.
- Refuse to spend a host clock STEP as a crystal rate — `hostAxis` divides a single multi-second jump by the whole span and hands back a fabricated ppm that was applied to `fs`; on 2026-08-26 one 3.0 s step quoted −10.02 ppm where the same device reads −19.8 to −23.0 on every other night.

### Changed
- Six bug classes proven on 2026-08-31 folded into the deep-audit charter, each with the PR that proved it.
- MUTATION-FLEET-EXPANSION closed for Phases 1 and 2, and its guard-count row corrected rather than left to be acted on. (`MUTATION-FLEET-EXPANSION-2026-08-25-BRIEF.md`)

### Fixed
- A single runaway mutant could hang the whole audit, because the only bound was per MODULE.
- The gate that keeps `capture.py` measurable had two holes, and both offenders it was built to catch walked through it.
- CPAP capture-host L/s unit hygiene: corrected the auto-stop log and standby comments that labeled L/s flow as L/min; migrated the misleading auto-stop config key `flow_eps_lpm` to `flow_eps_lps` (legacy key still accepted with the SAME value — never divided by 60 — plus a deprecation warning); and locked the MaskPressure (bus↔EDF) and PMD (`_LIVE_META`↔PSL-header) unit pairs with differential regressions so two representations of one channel cannot silently diverge.
- The refusal reported an assertion's headline and dropped its body — which is the half that says what failed.
- The mutation baseline ran a different selection than the mutants, so `capture.py` could never be measured at all.
- The mutation gate's refusal now names the test that caused it, instead of only that one did.
- CPAP flow bus label read L/min for L/s data — a ~60x unit mislabel on the monitor; corrected to L/s with a differential regression pinning it to the EDF flow unit. (`CPAP-EDF-WRITER-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`)
- The guarantee census reported "0 promises ungated" because its survivor file never parsed. (`CPAP-AUTOHARVEST-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-03-BRIEF.md`)
- The mutation job resolved a different shellcheck than the rest of CI, so its baseline failed on findings no other lane produces.
- The shell-surface tests walked mutmut's generated tree, which is why `capture.py` could never be mutation-tested.

- A branch code was called a firmware version, and a guard compared the wrong two fields.
- capture-host tests: a leaked module-global event can no longer hollow out a later test.
- A post-therapy CPAP harvest is now a durable job rather than a fired flag, so a restart mid-harvest re-queues the night instead of reporting it done: the therapy-end trigger persists `therapy_ended → harvest_requested → harvest_attempted → harvest_completed` (or `harvest_deferred`, with the reason) to an append-only fsynced `cpap-harvest-jobs.jsonl`, only a completion carrying a completion stamp can stop a future harvest, and every other state — including an unreadable record or one claiming completion with no stamp — re-queues. The 13:00 window is demoted from primary trigger to reconciliation: an outstanding job now drives the loop immediately, and the window asks the job store before paying for a card read so it cannot double-harvest. Two restart paths learned to wait for a harvest the way they already wait for a recording — `tepna-update.sh`'s interlock reports `harvesting` and defers with its own branch (the catch-all would have failed the unit for what is correct behaviour), and the monitor's restart/stop/reboot endpoint answers 409 with a `force` hatch. The legacy `cpap-therapy-end-fired.json` is migrated once, as a re-queue rather than a completion, and deleted, because it only ever recorded that a trigger fired. Measured cause of the incident behind this: 127 daemon restarts in six days, every one a clean exit, median 18/day, all deploys — one landed 108 s into a harvest and the card was not read for another 5.5 h.

- **A faulted O2Ring probe was being recorded as a worn finger, and an unplugged one was being read as
- The stored file type selects a COMMAND FAMILY; it was never a wire field. `file_start_frame`'s
- capture-host: a runner waiting to retry no longer publishes `connected: true`. (`CAPTURE-HOST-RESOURCE-ORCHESTRATION-AUDIT-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md`)
- The ring's ACC is a zero-order hold: it measures at 1.5625 Hz while the capture path writes ~9.979 Hz (`PPG-ABSENCE-AS-VALUE-2026-09-06-BRIEF.md`)
- The end-of-night class-B back-check scanned STATUS columns as waveforms.
- `equivalent` — the strongest class in the mutation equivalence ledger — was missing from `EXCUSING`, (`MUTATION-COVERAGE-SELECTION-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- `resume_strategy` has decided re-serve-vs-resume since G1 and was reachable only from the pure (`OXYII-G1-TRANSACTIONAL-SYNC-2026-08-23-BRIEF.md`)
- The O2Ring powers itself off about two minutes after it is taken off, and the daemon read that as an outage — a reconnect backoff spent against a radio that is off, and a false "capture is missing it" alert five minutes later, for a ring whose night had already been pulled. Measured over 244 harvested sessions: the ring's own idle timer runs 121.9 s (n=23, sd 1.18) from the last worn frame, while our not-worn drop fires at 47.9 s (n=18, sd 2.33) — and the two bands are separated in time, not mixed, every observation of the ring's timer falling on or before 2026-08-26 and every observation of our drop on or after 2026-08-27, which is what makes 121.9 s a hardware figure rather than a measurement of ourselves. A non-advertising ring whose stored session has been pulled is now named `ring powered off — idle timer`, expected until re-wear or charger, logged once instead of every backoff cycle and carrying no alert. Two bounds keep that from becoming a permanent silence: only a pull that actually COMPLETED licenses it, so a doff whose pull failed or ran partial still alerts with the night's data still on the device; and the state expires after 8 hours, after which a ring that is genuinely flat or forgotten goes back to alerting, because a false all-clear is worse than the false alarm it replaces.
- The monitor's `Streams · N of M live` counted streams that had ever produced data rather than streams that were flowing, so it read "2 of 12 live" with every device disconnected. The two were the O2Ring's `motion_o2` and `pi_o2`, which pushed before the ring dropped at 09:49 and kept the bus's `active` flag: that flag is documented as "streams that have produced data this session", is set on the first push, and is cleared only by `unregister` — which the O2Ring path never calls on disconnect. The stall was visible the whole time in a field the heading did not consult, `health`, which IS recomputed from the age of the last sample. The claim of liveness now excludes `health:'stall'`, and `weak` is counted among the live rather than alongside them, because a silent stream is not a degraded one and the previous code reported the same two streams as both live and weak in one heading. The denominator deliberately stays every declared stream, so a configured-and-failing stream is still visible rather than vanishing from the count.
- The monitor's timeline cache held exactly one entry and expired exactly as the page re-polled it, so most polls paid a full rebuild — 1.35 s for the night in progress and 2.05 s for a complete 1330 MB one, growing as the night grows. `_tl_cache.clear()` ran before every insert, defeating the "cached per (night, buckets)" the comment above it promised: a second viewer, or the same viewer on a different bucket count, evicted the other outright. The TTL was also 60 s against `monitor.html`'s 60 000 ms `loadTimeline` interval, so even the surviving entry expired as the next poll arrived. The cost is not server latency — the build is already off the event loop in a thread and `/api/state` holds at 3 ms throughout — it is that the page keeps up to 4 permanent SSE connections of a browser's ~6 per-host HTTP/1.1 budget, so a two-second request occupies one of the two remaining slots and the 1 s state poll queues behind it, which is what makes the page visibly stop. The cache now evicts the oldest entry past a bound of 8 rather than clearing, and the TTL is 300 s — chosen against the display (at the 600-bucket maximum over a ~10 h night one bucket is ~60 s wide, so sub-minute freshness cannot be rendered), not against the poll. Both bounds moved to module scope so a test can assert the TTL against the interval read out of the page itself.

Geometry measured against real 95 KB / 81 KB `.dat` files: the invariant is `(size-58)/3 == total_seconds`; the JS parser's `ff ff` end-marker sits ~10 records before the trailer and would mis-count, so the size/trailer arithmetic is the reliable one. Adds an `adapters:` name→MAC map, an optional per-device `adapter:` key, and `--instance` so `tepna-capture@sena` can serve only the devices pinned to that radio. `instance_devices(cfg, None)` returns EVERY device, so the split is opt-in per box and upgrading the code alone cannot silently strip devices from a running capture. The pin is a PREFERENCE, not an absolute (owner decision): an absent pinned radio degrades to the BlueZ default with a loud log rather than refusing to capture, because losing a night to a pin obeyed too literally is worse than a night on the wrong radio. `unowned_devices()` exists for the one failure no single instance can see — a device pinned to a radio nothing serves is captured by nobody while every instance logs a healthy startup — and it is an ERROR at startup, as is an unrecognised `--instance`, which would otherwise serve zero devices while looking like a working daemon. - The merge layer for the per-adapter daemon split (§3.6): each instance publishes `status.<instance>.json` with a `heartbeat_ms`, and `status_union.py` unions them for the monitor and nightqc. Nothing shares a file, so there is no locking and no writer contention. The union is taken over the EXPECTED instance set from config, never the found files — a dead instance is rendered `dead`/`stale` with its last-seen age rather than silently omitted, and the union carries its own `degraded` verdict so a consumer cannot render a healthy-looking view by accident. A status doc with no heartbeat is treated as dead, not live: an unaged status is one that cannot be shown to be current. An un-split box keeps writing plain `status.json` and reads back through the same union, so no consumer needs a flag-day. (`PER-DEVICE-ADAPTER-PINNING-2026-08-26-BRIEF.md`)

- `detectCVHR` refuses a beat series whose span is implausible instead of sizing its resample grid from it. `M = Math.floor(tEnd)` fed five `Float64Array`s **and** two `Array.from` copies with nothing bounding it; on a real 2026-08-23 night `tt[N-1]` was 241,259,871 s (7.6 years), so `M` was 241 million. The typed allocations all SUCCEEDED — they take external memory — and the failure surfaced only at `Array.from(sm)`, which must materialise a plain array and blew V8's cap with `RangeError: Invalid array length`, killing that night's entire ECGDex export. The diagnostic shape is a DISCONTINUITY, not a wrong sample rate: `tt[0]=0.023` and `tt[1]=0.346` are normal beat spacing and only the tail jumps, whereas a bad `fs` would have scaled `tt[1]` too. So this refuses rather than repairs — we cannot know which fragment carries the right timebase, and a 1 Hz series spanning years is not something to resample. Bound is 48 h, over twice any real recording, so a heavily-gapped night still fits; `index: null` (never 0, which would read as "we looked for cyclic variation and found none" — the fabricated negative the Clock Contract §2.6 forbids) plus `reason: 'implausible-span'`. Gate-backed by an assertion built on the measured geometry, verified against a neutralised-guard decoy that reproduced the original blow-up at 8.4 GB RSS rather than merely failing.
- `parse_hciconfig` now drops a controller whose BD address is the null (`00:00:00:00:00:00`) or broadcast (`FF:FF:FF:FF:FF:FF`) address, so `failover_target` can never hand a reconnect an address no device is reachable on. The upstream shape test — 17 characters, 5 colons — checks the FORMAT only, and the null address satisfies it, which defeated `parse_hciconfig`'s own stated contract that *"a block with no MAC is dropped — an adapter we cannot address is not a failover target."* Not hypothetical: the Zephyr/nRF52840 dongle (USB `2fe3:000b`) reports exactly that, because its firmware has no PUBLIC address (Zephyr identifies by static-random and refuses a host-side public pin with `0x0c Not Supported`) — BlueZ shows the static-random address while `hciconfig`, the layer this parser reads, shows zeros. It survived the parse, and `failover_target` returns the FIRST match in an order that is not sorted, so a wedged radio would "fail over" onto an unreachable address — silence dressed as recovery, and strictly worse than staying on the wedged adapter. Tests are built from the dongle's real `hciconfig` output and verified to fail without the guard.
- Harden both realm execution wrappers against async results. resultString and runBattery caught only

- `tools/beat-error-recovery.mjs` — target 6 of the known-clock experiment: inject beat-detection errors of known type and rate into a real RR train, run the shipped HRV path, measure the damage. Self-tested, seeded, deterministic. Run over 101 real H10 nights (median 1440 beats, median true rMSSD 44.6 ms). **The headline: one missed beat in a thousand inflates rMSSD by 20.8 %**, while mean RR moves 0.06 % — so a summary HR looks perfect while the HRV metric is a fifth wrong. For scale, a 30 ppm clock error over a 7 h night moves rMSSD by ~0.003 %, because rMSSD is a first difference and a smooth rate error cancels; a missed beat fuses two intervals and rMSSD is quadratic in that. Detector accuracy and clock accuracy are not comparable quantities and the suite had been measuring the smaller one. At heavier corruption the raw inflation reaches +387 % (2 % miss) and +614 % (5 % miss). Malik correction removes the injected damage but lands on the SAME value regardless of what was injected (−22 % at both 0.1 % and 5 % miss), i.e. it converges on its own flattened estimate rather than on truth. **What the experiment cannot decide, and the brief says so:** the NULL row's −21.95 % is either a corrector bias or the correct removal of ~22 % of genuine artefact inflation in the as-recorded train — separating them needs independently adjudicated R-peaks, which this corpus lacks. Do not cite it as "Malik is biased". What it does establish is magnitude: correcting a median of 3 beats in 1440 moves rMSSD 22 %, because rMSSD's extremes dominate it. Also measures the cost of using the WRONG corrector — the suite intentionally applies 0.20 to ECG/Pulse and 0.30 to optical PPG — and reports both legs side by side. Records a third instance of this session's recurring tool bug: the tool read `correctRR`'s return as `rr|corrected|out` when the shipped shape is `{nn,tt,nCorr,flags}`, so the first corpus run printed "after Malik: n/a" across all 101 streams while the corrector ran fine. Regression assertions now require both corrector legs to be populated. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Closes the last measurable target of the known-clock experiment: an END-TO-END timing test for the capture write path. Phases 1-3 perturb the arrival SIDECAR, which leaves the capture daemon outside the loop — decoding a PMD frame, stamping arrival and formatting the CSV all happen before the substrate those experiments touch, so an acquisition-side defect was invisible to every one of them. `capture-host` holds a 100 % coverage floor and had **no test following a clock relationship from a wire frame to a recovered rate**. `capture-host/tests/test_write_path_clock_recovery.py` plants a known device-vs-host rate as raw PMD bytes, decodes it with the REAL `decode_frame`, writes it with the REAL `PmdArrivalLogWriter`, reads the file back and recovers the rate from the written columns — nothing reimplemented, because a test that formats its own CSV passes while the shipped formatter is broken. Planted 0 / +50 / −50 / +200 ppm over a 40-minute session at 130 Hz; each recovers within 2 ppm at the correct sign (host−device, so a fast device reads negative). Two properties are pinned separately because the rate test does not cover them: the arrival stamp keeps MILLISECOND resolution (a whole-second stamp would make every rate under ~400 ppm unrecoverable while the 200 ppm case still passed), and a missing device stamp is written blank rather than as a fabricated 0. **The tests were verified by breaking the code rather than by passing**: truncating `_phone_ts` to whole seconds kills 3 of 4, and making the blank-field helper emit "0" kills the fourth; both mutants were reverted and `writers.py` is byte-identical. A first-run pass is not evidence here — the repo's own lesson is that a gate counts only once you have seen it fail. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- The capture-host restart helper gains a `reload` verb, and the monitor a **Reload units** button, so a
- A **Deploy now** button in the monitor, and `tepna-update.sh`'s first-ever argument.
- `hostAxis` can now tell a device column that is a clock from one that was drawn. Found by running the known-clock experiment against a real three-device box night: `independent` was `spreadMs > 2 ms`, which detects the OPPOSITE of what it must for a fabricated axis — a counter synthesised as `index x an assumed rate` at 1 s granularity produces an enormous residual spread, so the coarser the fabrication the more independent it read. Measured on the O2Ring's real monotonic run: `ok:true, ppm = 2765.5, independent:true` — a confident rate for a device with no oscillator, consumed downstream by `pat-gate.js`, `ecgdex-dsp.js`'s fs correction and `integrator-dsp.js`'s skew decision. The whole-file run refused only by luck (a mid-session counter reset makes the device span negative, tripping the plausibility bound); on the monotonic segment a node actually parses, it sails through. `hostAxis` now computes the modal-delta concentration from the anchors it already holds and publishes `drawnShare`, `deviceDrawn` and `drawnReason`. The bound `0.67` is measured, not chosen: over 381 arrival sidecars the 356 real clock streams top out at **56.00 %** and the 25 drawn ones bottom out at **79.04 %**, with nothing between — 0/25 missed, 0/356 false positives. This also shows the pre-existing >=99 % drawn test misses **5 of those 25**, since one counter reset or repeated stamp drops a genuinely fabricated stream to 79 %. `deviceDrawn` is published ADDITIVELY and deliberately does NOT gate `independent`: folding it in was implemented and reverted because it reds 11 assertions including ECGDex's planted-drift recovery, whose fixture legitimately uses a uniform device axis — the detector is right about real data and wrong to be load-bearing against a fixture. The remaining hole is asserted explicitly in the new test group so it cannot be closed silently without migrating consumers. Also recorded: `Verity ppi` is 100 % drawn, a second fabricated stream not previously flagged. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- `tools/hostaxis-estimator-bakeoff.mjs` — the planted-recovery experiment behind `hostAxis`'s (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- `tools/known-clock-recovery.mjs` — inject a clock defect of known size into a real recording, run the shipped estimator, measure what comes back. Deterministic by contract (no `Math.random`, no `Date.now`; the one noise source is a seeded LCG), self-tested, and run over 395 arrival sidecars across 21 nights and 3 devices. Paper: `papers/known-clock-recovery.html`. Validated the stability instrument against an OUTSIDE reference first — `capture-host/allan.py` had only ever been known-answer tested against its own synthesised noise, which proves self-consistency and not correctness; against `allantools` 2024.06 it agrees to **4.78e-14** worst per-tau relative difference across all four noise types. **Results:** frequency recovery is unbiased and linear across +/-1 to +/-500 ppm at 0.03-0.09 % median relative error; a constant offset recovers as exactly 0.000 ppm on all 61 streams, so a ppm carries NO information about absolute alignment; contiguous packet loss of 50 % moves the rate by exactly 0.0000 ppm while interleaved decimation moves it up to 1115 ppm. **Two sensitivity floors found:** a 200 ms timestamp step is invisible (median ratio 1.0x), and injected wander of 0.5-5 ppm/step never moves the Allan slope off tau^-1. **The central result:** sigma_y(tau) at each recording's own span EXCEEDS the rate being quoted for 100 % of streams under 300 s, 94 % under an hour and 42 % beyond it — the estimator's accuracy and the quantity's resolvability are independent questions and only the first had been asked. **[WITHDRAWN 2026-08-14 — see `2026-08-14-span-gate-withdrawn.md`: the gate is doing net good where it stands, helping on 82 % of cases at 2400 s.]** This changeset originally inferred that `ECG_AXIS_MIN_SPAN_MS = 2400 s` is ~an order of magnitude too permissive; that conflated whether a rate is RESOLVABLE with whether correcting REDUCES ERROR. Also records two bugs found in the new tool itself by measuring rather than reasoning: a guard on `stability.ok`, a field that does not exist, which made the whole first corpus run report wander as "(none)" — the repo's own named failure class, committed by the instrument built to hunt it — and a sign error in the self-test that failed at exactly -198.7 %, the injected sign doubled. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Records the owner's known-clock adversarial capture design, and the four measured facts that change it. The framing the brief leads with: every timing number the suite publishes is **self-consistent but unanchored** — `hostAxis` measures device against host, Allan measures host against itself, closure measures three sources against each other, and nothing has ever been compared to a truth known in advance — so this is the only experiment in the backlog that can fail in a way the others structurally cannot. **[WITHDRAWN 2026-08-15 — see `2026-08-15-stratum-claim-withdrawn.md`: the box is source-stratum 1 and PASSES the gate; `chronyc`'s tracking-stratum is normalised by −1 before the comparison.]** This changeset originally stated that the box is Stratum 2 and therefore fails `host_clock.py`'s own `TIMEBASE_MAX_STRATUM = 1` rate-trust gate. What survives is that its 19.5 µs RMS offset is ~10⁴ tighter than the BLE jitter that immediately follows, so PPS-disciplining buys precision the transport destroys; the **O2Ring cannot be a third clock leg** because its axis is drawn, which makes it the experiment's negative control (every recovery target must refuse on it); **constant offset is not identifiable from beat matching** (alignment holds only mod one RR) so its criterion must rest on an aperiodic marker; and the box has **2 BLE adapters for 3 devices**, confounding any sensor-specific noise term with adapter assignment. Adds a preregistration protocol (`acceptance.json` committed before the first blind run) and a mandatory pre-committed null night, since a pipeline that recovers a perturbation from an unperturbed night has failed and nothing else in the design catches it. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Spawns the follow-up brief for the known-clock experiment, per the house rule that an executed brief records what execution discovered. Its purpose is to make the parent's incompleteness VISIBLE rather than implied: four phases and a paper landed in one day, and three of seven acceptance items are unmet. Two of those are not analysis problems — target 1 (constant offset) has never actually been tested, because the parent measured only that the estimator is blind to it by construction, and testing it needs an aperiodic cross-device marker that no recording on disk contains; and adapter assignment is unrecorded, so noise can be attributed to a stream but not to a device. Both are capture-protocol changes, which is why running further analyses would add results without closing the holes. Also carries the deliberately-open consumer migration (`hostAxis` publishes `deviceDrawn` but does not gate `independent` on it, since folding it in reds ECGDex's planted-drift recovery whose fixture legitimately uses a uniform device axis; four consumers still read the old flag, `integrator-dsp.js` being the highest-harm one), the blind-operator gap that §3.6b's withdrawal demonstrates cannot self-fix, and four smaller items recorded so they are not rediscovered. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Closes targets 7 and 8 of the known-clock experiment and completes the paper as Draft v2, retitled because the evidence moved the thesis. **Target 8 — host-induced artifacts, measured rather than argued.** §2.1 had reasoned from a ratio that the host could not be the limiting term; that is an argument, not a measurement. A host disturbance is COMMON-MODE across two devices captured simultaneously by one host, so the two detrended residuals were correlated on a shared grid: median **r = 0.036** over 4 pairs (median 4.8 h overlap), i.e. ~3.6 % of residual variance is common-mode and the rest is device or transport. Residual SD is 42.6 ms (H10) / 48.9 ms (Verity) against a 19.5 us host RMS offset — **0.046 %** of what it would have to explain. **Target 7 — sensor-specific noise, with the adapter confound escaped.** The brief had recorded this target as blocked because device noise is confounded with which radio carried it. Comparing two streams from the SAME device on the SAME adapter on the SAME night at matched span removes that: at 4800 s the Verity accelerometer reads **322.8 ppm** sigma_y against the same device's optical stream at **42.0** — **7.7x**, and by construction not an adapter term. Its Allan slope is also the least steep (−0.971 vs −0.990), so it averages down slightly less well. All streams remain phase-noise dominated. **Paper v2** adds §3.8 (beat errors), §3.9 (host common-mode), §3.10 (stream-specific noise), rewrites the abstract, limitations and conclusion, and is retitled from "The estimator is fine; the quantity is not" to "We were measuring the wrong error" — because the beat substrate showed a 20.8 % rMSSD cost for a 1-in-1000 missed beat against ~0.003 % for the entire clock-error family, and a paper whose headline understates its own result is mis-titled. Limitations now state every small-n sub-analysis explicitly (9–11 streams for the span test, 4 pairs for host common-mode, 4–6 per row for stream noise) and note that the same operator injected and analysed. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- The monitor gains the three recovery rungs a human has to decide: **Restart bluetooth**, **Re-bind
- `tools/tch-bootstrap-ci.mjs` — the first confidence intervals for the three-cornered-hat sigmas, which (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- `tools/aperiodic-offset.mjs` and a **negative result** on target 1. The follow-up brief recorded that testing a constant offset needs an aperiodic cross-device marker and therefore a capture-protocol change. I proposed a shortcut — nature already supplies one, since turning over in bed produces a transient in BOTH devices' accelerometers at the same instant — and tested it on the real paired night (H10 chest vs Verity arm, 2026-08-13, 4.75 h overlap, ~868k samples each). **There is no usable shared transient.** Peak prominence is 0.0017–0.018 against a posture-only NULL control of 0.002, i.e. indistinguishable, and the peak RIDES THE SEARCH BOUNDARY — 3850 ms at ±4 s, 5750 at ±6 s, 9000 at ±9 s — which is what an argmax of noise does and a real lock never does. Chest and arm accelerometers do not see the same impulse well enough at this granularity over a sleep night. The brief's original claim stands and the shortcut does not exist. **The trap this found is worth more than the hypothesis was: testing an offset estimator by injecting a constant offset is VACUOUS.** A constant shift translates the whole correlation surface rigidly, so the argmax moves by exactly the injected amount regardless of whether it means anything — measured, injections of −3000/−1000/+1000/+3000 ms were recovered with error EXACTLY 0, four for four, on a night with no lock at all. Reported without prominence and the range test beside them, those numbers would have read as a clean validation of target 1; they are the most convincing instance yet of a check reporting success about something it never examined, precisely because the arithmetic is flawless. Both discriminators — prominence against a null control, and invariance of the peak to the search range — are now asserted in `--self-test`, and the tool is kept as the instrument that would detect a marker if a future capture deliberately made one. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- `tools/beat-capture-recapture.mjs` — §7's capture-recapture, built and run on three shipped detectors over one host axis (18.85 min of the 2026-08-13 box night): ECG 970 beats, Verity PPG 963, O2Ring PPG 952, all ~51 bpm. **Pulse arrival time falls out as a by-product and is physiologically ordered — median 337.9 ms to the arm, 410.2 ms to the finger** — and aligning on it is mandatory rather than cosmetic: before it was handled the two optical sources matched each other 895 times and the ECG 10 times, giving `observed` = 1899 against ~970 real beats, every beat counted twice. **The estimator then REFUSES, and that refusal is the finding.** The closed form divides by m110·m101·m011 = 24·2·3 = 144 and multiplies by 9·1·12, giving m000 = 701 — 41 % of beats "missed by everything" — which would have been reported as a number had the guard not been added. It now applies the textbook adequacy rule (cell >= 5) to the six informative cells and says "the data cannot identify the undercount", explicitly not "nothing was missed". What this establishes: on a clean low-motion window the three detectors agree on 935 of 986 beats, and that agreement is exactly what destroys the estimator's power, because the cells carrying information about the unseen are single-digit. **The method has power precisely where the data is bad** — motion, perfusion, apnea — which is where the missed beats that matter for rMSSD live; quiet sleep was the wrong first test and the refusal is what said so. Getting here took four wrong diagnoses of a "0 beats" result — primitive-vs-pipeline, segment length, realm state leakage, and finally the actual cause: `detectChannel` places feet at SUB-SAMPLE fractional indices, so `t[i]` was `undefined` and a `Number.isFinite` filter silently discarded every detection. Host times are now interpolated between bracketing samples, which also keeps the sub-sample precision rather than rounding it away. Each wrong turn is recorded in the code where the reader will meet it. (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- `--blind-prepare` / `--blind-score` turn "hand the analysis to another operator" from an intention into two commands. This work preregistered its criteria and still withdrew two claims, both caught by the same operator running one more check — the mechanism that cannot be relied on — and an instruction nobody follows is not a control. Prepare writes `blinded.json` (anchor pairs and opaque ids) plus a sealed `TRUTH.json`; the analyst reports `{id -> recoveredPpm}` from the blinded file alone; score compares. **What is hidden is stated plainly**: device, night, whether a perturbation was applied at all, its family and its magnitude — including whether the draw is a NULL. The menu is deliberately published (an analyst can read the source); the secret is the DRAW, and the key is caller-supplied so the tool holds no hidden state and a disputed result can be re-derived rather than re-argued. Audited on the real 35.7 MB artefact: zero matches for device names, nights, draw ids or magnitude fields. **Two harness defects were found by running it rather than reasoning about it, and both are recorded in the code.** (1) The truth was stored as a DELTA while the analyst can only report an ABSOLUTE rate — they never receive a baseline to subtract — which scored 21 of 61 streams as false positives when every one was the estimator working correctly; the baseline is now computed at prepare time, where the unperturbed anchors are still in hand. (2) A frequency draw cannot be applied to a zero-span stream, so expecting a recovery there demands the impossible: `freq-pos-mid` scored a median error of exactly 100.000 ppm — the injected magnitude — because 10 draws landed on the corpus's zero-span drawn streams. Those are now marked inapplicable and reported as a count, never silently dropped. End-to-end on the corpus: 51 answered, 10 inapplicable, median |err| 0.000 ppm, worst 1.025, zero false positives on null draws, reproducing the open-label offset and packet-loss findings blind. Both legs were run by the same operator, so this is NOT itself a blind result — it proves the mechanism and removes the excuse. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Adds §7 to the cross-domain follow-ups: epidemiology's capture-recapture, imported to answer the largest open finding from `KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE` — **how many beats did every detector miss?** That question gates the whole suite, because one missed beat in a thousand inflates rMSSD by 20.8 % against ~0.003 % for the entire clock-error family, and it is also the only route to deciding whether Malik correction's −22 % on a clean train is bias or correct repair. Current practice in the field (the PPG-beats toolbox) establishes reference beats as those TWO ECG detectors both found — an intersection, which by construction discards the beats both missed, i.e. exactly the population being sought; sound as a quality filter, biased as truth. Lincoln–Petersen estimates that unobserved class from the overlap. **The decisive constraint, checked rather than assumed:** the estimator requires independent capture, our detectors fail together (motion, perfusion, apnea degrade optical and electrical at once), and positive dependence biases the estimate DOWNWARD — it would under-report the very undercount it is measuring. With two lists the model is saturated so the dependence cannot be estimated at all; the remedy is a log-linear model with interaction terms, needing ≥3 sources. We have three genuinely different instruments (H10 electrical/chest, Verity optical/arm, O2Ring optical/finger). **Measured 2026-08-15 and it eliminates a fourth candidate:** the Verity firmware `_RR.txt` is EMPTY — 0 beats over the same night the H10 firmware reports 15,522 — confirming CLAUDE.md's documented all-zero onboard files on this night, so a Verity beat must come from raw PPG; and H10-firmware vs H10-Pan–Tompkins are not independent, being the same waveform, so they cannot be two of the three. Done-when requires the interaction terms to be REPORTED, since a fit finding no dependence on this data would be the surprising result and must not pass silently. (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Adds `--scan` to `tools/beat-capture-recapture.mjs` and records the result, which closes this line for the current corpus. Windows are ranked by DISAGREEMENT COUNT — a property of the three beat sets computed before any estimate exists, i.e. selection on precision and never on the estimate, the miss rate, or whether the estimator returned ok. Over the same 18.8-minute night: 9 windows, **median disagreement per window = 0**, and **0 of 9 identifiable**. In seven of nine windows the three detectors agree on EVERY beat; all disagreement is confined to two windows (26 and 22 beats) and even there the informative cells stay single-digit. **The limit this exposes is structural, not a shortage of data:** perfect agreement has two causes that capture-recapture cannot separate — nothing was missed, or all three detectors missed the same beats — and the second is exactly the positive-dependence case the method was adopted to handle. In the limit of total dependence the overlap carries no information, which the tool's own self-test already pins. Quiet sleep does not merely lack power; the question is unidentifiable there by construction. The honest next method is a different one: astronomy's artificial-star test and gravitational-wave injection campaigns face the same no-ground-truth problem and solve it the other way round — plant synthetic events of known amplitude into the real recording and measure what the shipped detector recovers, giving completeness as a function of SNR. That needs no second detector, no independence assumption and no disagreement, and it measures the quantity `beat-error-recovery.mjs` currently has to assume, since that tool perturbs an existing beat train rather than the waveform the detector actually reads. Recorded as the recommendation rather than built; capture-recapture remains correct for artefact-heavy segments where detectors genuinely diverge. (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- **`claude-md-claims` — CLAUDE.md's factual claims are now checked against the tree.**
- A `git commit` whose staged `*.js`/`*.mjs` are not Biome-clean is now denied, with the one-line fix. (`CAPTURE-HOST-SUBPROCESS-SURFACE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`)
- `tools/commit-shape.mjs` — the agent-neutral half of the shared-tree guards, wired into the `static` (`AGENT-NEUTRAL-GUARDS-2026-08-15-BRIEF.md`)
- `tools/beat-injection-recovery.mjs` — astronomy's artificial-star test, imported for beat detection, and the first bounded answer to "how many beats does the shipped detector miss?" Plants the subject's OWN averaged beat into the raw ECG at physiologically admissible positions (>=350 ms from any existing beat, so refractory rejection is never counted as a miss), re-runs the SHIPPED Pan-Tompkins, and measures recovered fraction against amplitude in units of LOCAL noise. On 25.6 min of H10 ECG (1315 baseline beats) the completeness curve has a sharp knee — 0 % at SNR <=10, **8.7 % at 20, 94.7 % at 30**, 100 % at >=40 — with **zero spurious detections at every amplitude**. Real beats on the same recording have median SNR **48.9** (p1 22.2, p5 41.0, min 10.0), with 0.61 % below SNR 20 and 2.21 % below 30; convolving distribution with curve gives roughly **1.4 % of beats at risk**. **That is an UPPER bound and the reason is structural:** injected beats sit in gaps, isolated from rhythm, while a real low-amplitude beat arrives in sequence where Pan-Tompkins' searchback reopens the window on a long RR — a mechanism built for exactly this case that an isolated plant cannot benefit from. The number must be quoted as a bound with its side named, never as the miss rate. It still matters, because a 0.5 % miss rate inflates rMSSD 114 % and 2 % inflates it 387 %: an upper bound of 1.4 % does not settle the question, it places it inside the range where the damage is large. Closing it needs the searchback-aware variant — remove a real beat and re-insert it at reduced amplitude so sequence context survives — which is a small next increment. Unlike `beat-error-recovery.mjs`, which perturbs an already-detected RR series and must therefore ASSUME a miss rate, this injects into the signal the detector actually reads, so the rate is measured. Self-test pins the curve's monotonicity and a NULL injection recovering nothing; the first version probed at SNR 20 and read a correct 0 % as a broken harness, which is recorded in the test. (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

It is now in both roster tables, with its two genuine gaps stated rather than papered over: no reference guide, no landing-page tile. **`capture-host/` was also absent** — 189 Python files and a separate gate (`ruff` · `shellcheck` · `pytest` at a 100 % statement-and-branch floor over 3,200+ tests) that `npm run check` does not cover — so the Health Box now has a section saying it is a producer outside the browser suite and gated on its own lane. Replaces the `"75+ metrics"` framing with the measured count and its tier distribution (404 across the 8 node registries: 118 measured · 84 validated · 78 emerging · 96 experimental · 28 heuristic), which makes the surface-area-vs-confidence argument concrete instead of rhetorical. Also corrects the test figures against a real green run — **4,650+ assertions / 305 groups → 7,333 assertions / 470 groups** (`node tests/run-tests.mjs`, exit 0, 12 skipped) — the no-network scope (8 → 9 provenance-gated bundles), the doc count (~180 → ~400 briefs), and refreshes the momentum note from v2.0.0 to the 2.1→2.5 mutation-testing and host-axis arc. Every figure was re-measured from the tree, and the paper's headline numbers (47.7 ppm, 11 nights, 145,586 coupled beats) were re-verified against `papers/wearable-clock-drift.html` rather than carried forward.

- Closes the drawn-axis consumer migration, and **two of the four consumers were closed by declining to change them**. The flag was wired exactly where a drawn axis can arrive, and the corpus says where that is: O2Ring 20/20 drawn and Verity `ppi` 1/1, against H10 ecg **0/6** and Verity ppg **0/19**. `integrator-dsp.js` (#1274) had a measured specimen — one real O2Ring segment reporting −22.83 ppm at a 99.3 % drawn share, passing both existing guards. `tools/pat-host-offset.mjs` (#1278) takes the Wellue finger PPG as its PAT target, which is the drawn device itself. `ecgdex-dsp.js` is **deliberately not gated**: zero measured exposure, and adding the guard would force re-cutting a legitimate planted-drift fixture whose uniform device axis is by construction indistinguishable from a fabricated one. `pat-gate.js` is **deliberately not gated**: nothing live passes it an axis — `PAT Feasibility.html` calls neither `verdict(` nor `hostAxis(`. Both would have been machinery for a hypothetical, and adding them for symmetry would make the guard look thorough while teaching a reader the flag matters where it does not; the brief records that either decision reopens the moment its premise changes. Also records what the adapter-assignment item actually requires: `capture.py` holds a single global `ADAPTER` for bonding, not a per-device assignment, so BlueZ selects the controller and "record which radio carried which link" is a design change (the BlueZ object path carries it at connect time) plus hardware validation on a nightly-capturing box — not a log line, and not to be made blind. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- Paper Draft v3 adds §3.2b: the second route to a constant offset also fails, and the obvious test for it cannot. §3.2 already showed the shipped estimator is blind to constant offset BY CONSTRUCTION; the standard alternative is to align an aperiodic feature, and we asked whether a night already contains one — a subject turning over produces a transient in both a chest and an arm accelerometer at the same instant. On 4.75 h of paired overnight ACC (~868,000 samples per device) it is not usable: peak prominence 0.0017–0.018 against a posture-only null control of 0.002, and the peak MOVES WITH THE SEARCH RANGE (3850 ms at ±4 s, 5750 at ±6 s, 9000 at ±9 s), which a genuine lock never does. **The transferable result is the validation trap.** The natural test — inject a known constant offset, check it is recovered — cannot fail, because a constant shift translates the entire cross-correlation surface rigidly and the peak moves by exactly the injected amount whether or not it locates anything: four injections were recovered with error exactly 0 from a method with no lock at all. Reported alone those numbers are indistinguishable from a validated alignment and MORE persuasive than a noisy correct result. The paper now states that an injected constant offset is not evidence for a correlation-based offset estimator, and names the two things that are: prominence against a null control, and invariance of the peak to the search range. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Paper Draft v4 — the beat detector itself, which the paper had measured the CONSEQUENCES of (§3.8: one missed beat in a thousand costs 20.8 % of rMSSD) without ever measuring the rate. Adds §3.11, two no-ground-truth methods imported from other fields. **Capture-recapture (epidemiology) does not work here and the reason is the finding:** three independent detectors on one recording agreed on 935 of 986 beats, the informative cells collapsed to single digits, and the closed form returned 41 % of the recording as unseen; it now refuses, reporting that the data cannot identify the undercount and explicitly not that nothing was missed. Ranking windows by disagreement did not rescue it — in seven of nine windows all three detectors agreed on every beat, and perfect agreement is equally consistent with nothing missed and with all three missing the same beats, so the quantity is unidentifiable by construction on quiet recordings. **The artificial-star test (observational astronomy) does work:** planting the subject's own averaged beat into the raw waveform at admissible positions gives a completeness curve with a sharp threshold at 20-30x local noise and zero induced false positives, placing ~1 % of real beats at risk. Adds §3.12, the correction to that: attenuating real beats IN SITU shows gap-planting OVERSTATES the detector by ~3x (1.56 % vs 0.53 % on a common axis) — the opposite sign to the caveat we published — driven by the adaptive threshold rather than searchback, isolated by attenuating a beat together with its neighbours (recovery 1.2 % -> ~74 %) and with the morphology alternative excluded by a second construction agreeing to ~11 %. Records the measurement artefact found in the process: template subtraction leaves a beat-shaped, beat-POSITIONED residual, so the nominal amplitude axis reported silence where the detector still saw signal, identified because detections at nominal zero sat a median 15.4 ms from the true beat rather than at the ~507 ms interval midpoint an inference would produce. §3.12's experiment was performed independently by another session and is reported with its numbers unchanged and credited to `SEARCHBACK-AWARE-INJECTION-2026-08-15-BRIEF.md`. Abstract, limitations, conclusion, reproduction block and references updated; Chao 1987 and the artificial-star design added as sources. (`CROSS-DOMAIN-METHODS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- Withdraws a claim this session shipped one release earlier: that `ECG_AXIS_MIN_SPAN_MS = 2400 s` is "roughly an order of magnitude too permissive". It is not, and the correction is recorded in the brief and the paper rather than deleted, because it shipped in both. The error was conflating two questions that a single ratio cannot separate: whether a rate is **resolved** (|ppm| vs sigma_y — quotable as distinguishable from zero, which under an hour it is not) and whether applying the correction **reduces error** (|estimate − truth| vs |0 − truth|). A span gate governs the second. A point estimate can sit below its own noise floor and still be much closer to the truth than assuming zero. Measured directly, taking the full-span rate as truth on streams whose full-span estimate is itself resolved and then truncating the same stream: at the shipped 2400 s gate the correction **helps on 82 %** of cases and more than halves the median error (8.41 ppm corrected vs 22.27 uncorrected); at 4800 s and 9600 s it helps 100 %. The gate is doing net good where it stands; a raise to 4800 s would remove the 18 % harm cases (worst: Verity truth −34.3 ppm estimated at −77.3) at the cost of refusing correction on fragments it currently improves — defensible, and a far smaller claim. n is 9–11 because truth requires a resolved full-span estimate. `HOSTAXIS-STABILITY` made a claim of this shape and withdrew it as "marginal, not wrong"; this work re-opened it on stronger evidence and then independently reproduced the reason it was withdrawn — the withdrawal was correct. The sigma_y result is untouched: it licenses a caveat on QUOTING a ppm, not a change to a gate that governs APPLYING one. Adds the two harnesses that produced this (`experiments/known-clock/span-sweep.mjs`, `does-correction-help.mjs`), including a 162-point within-stream span sweep showing sigma_y halves as span doubles and resolution crossing 50 % between 4800 s and 9600 s. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- Found by auditing this session's own output against `main` rather than trusting it: **two withdrawn claims survived as live assertions in PENDING changesets**, which `tools/release.mjs` folds into `CHANGELOG.md` verbatim. The corrections had been applied to the briefs, the paper and the DOCS-INDEX rows — the obvious artefacts — and the changesets were missed, so the next release would have published "the box is **Stratum 2** and therefore fails its own rate-trust gate" and "`ECG_AXIS_MIN_SPAN_MS = 2400 s` is ~an order of magnitude too permissive" as statements of fact, with their retractions appearing as separate, later entries a reader has no reason to connect. Both are now marked inline with a `[WITHDRAWN …]` prefix naming the changeset that retracts them and the one-line reason (the box is source-stratum 1 because `chronyc`'s tracking-stratum is normalised by −1; the span gate helps on 82 % of cases at 2400 s, because "is the rate resolvable" and "does correcting reduce error" are different questions). Nothing is deleted — a changeset is a historical record and the claim is part of what happened; what changes is that the correction now travels ADJACENT to the claim instead of several entries away. **The general point, which is the reason this is worth a changeset of its own:** a withdrawal is only complete when it reaches every surface that will republish the claim, and a pending changeset is such a surface precisely because it has not been published yet. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- **The charging guard was correct, wired, and structurally unreachable in the one scenario it was written
- A `CLAIM` marker carrying a decimal was silently truncated. It is now rejected by name. (`DEV-TOOLCHAIN-2026-06-30-BRIEF.md`)
- Corrects a wrong mechanism this session wrote into both briefs, after the owner challenged it: that target 1's marker could be "a tap artefact … a motion impulse visible in both ACC streams". A tap on one device is a LOCAL mechanical event — to reach a device on another body segment it must travel through tissue, which damps, delays and reorients it. The measurement already in the brief refutes it: a whole-body roll, far larger than any tap, produces no alignable shared transient. **The marker must be shared by CO-LOCATION, not by transmission** — strap the two devices together so they are momentarily one rigid body, deliver one sharp impulse so both accelerometers experience literally the same acceleration, then don them. The clapperboard principle; nothing has to propagate. Adds §2.1a, an executable six-step protocol (both links streaming first; devices rigidly together; sharp rap rather than hard; 3–5 impulses so the lag can be checked ACROSS them; don; repeat the sequence before doffing so two brackets separate offset from drift) with an explicit acceptance rule: `locked` true AND lag agreeing across impulses AND invariant to the search range — any one alone is satisfiable by noise. Adds §2.1b, the evidence that no natural marker exists, which is stronger than the prominence figures alone: scanning all 285 one-minute windows of the paired night gives a median prominence of 0.0092, only 5 windows above the 0.05 lock bound, and their recovered lags spread over 4.5 seconds (+200, −50, −1700, +2800, −1300, +550 ms). A real constant offset would give the SAME lag in every locking window, so the failure is not coarse windowing or a weak detector — there is nothing to find, and re-analysis will not produce it. Also notes the corpus cannot test co-location, since the devices were worn throughout and never co-located: that needs one deliberate capture. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- Closes the highest-harm item of the known-clock follow-ups: `arrivalPairOffsets` — the function deciding whether two devices sit on ONE timebase — now refuses a drawn axis on PROVENANCE rather than on magnitude. Neither existing guard is a provenance test. `independent` compares two COLUMNS, and a counter synthesised as `index x an assumed rate` at 1 s granularity has an enormous residual spread, so it reads MORE independent the coarser the fabrication. `plausibleCrystal` is `|ppm| <= 200`, a MAGNITUDE proxy that catches the O2Ring only because that axis happens to report 2730 ppm — `trio-batch.mjs`'s own comment already said the flag "says nothing about whether the DEVICE column is a clock at all", so the intent was right and the implementation was a stand-in. **Measured over 395 sidecars: one real O2Ring segment (2026-08-13, 1.72 h) reports −22.83 ppm at a 99.3 % drawn-delta share — a textbook-plausible crystal sitting between the H10's −20 and the Verity's −34 — and passes BOTH guards while having no oscillator at all.** That is the case a magnitude test cannot catch by construction. `deviceDrawn`/`drawnShare` are now carried from `hostAxis` through `trio-batch.mjs` into the device record, the Integrator gate refuses on them, the refusal names the delta concentration, and `trio-batch`'s own usable-clock count excludes them. **`arrivalPairOffsets` had NO tests at all** despite being exported and making a cross-device decision; a group now pins it, verified by removing the provenance clause — 5 of 8 assertions fail without it, decisively "with two real clocks + one drawn, exactly TWO are usable — got 3". Reported honestly at n = 1: 16 other drawn streams also pass both guards but are zero-span, where ppm = 0 trivially. Note this does NOT change `hostAxis.independent`, which remains spread-only; the parent brief's KNOWN HOLE assertion stays true and correct — the consumer has migrated off the flag rather than the flag being redefined. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- `land-pr.mjs` waited on checks that cannot block a merge, and it cost **~90 minutes** on 2026-08-14.
- **`npm run lint` is now `biome ci` — it checks formatting, which it did not.**
- `capture-host/tools/mutate_diff.py` **refuses instead of reporting green when it cannot actually run (`PAT-OFFSET-ESTIMATOR-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-12-BRIEF.md`)
- Records that the O2Ring already has a commanded sync, which reframes §2.1a's co-location proposal — owner-supplied, and confirmed on hardware in `O2RING-OPCODE-SURFACE`. `0x83` drives the vibration motor (fired 5x, felt 5x, contact held) and `0x03` drains the PPG buffer, giving an exact sample COUNT between two host-timestamped reads. `0x83` beats co-location for this device because the impulse is generated INSIDE the device whose axis needs anchoring: nothing has to propagate through tissue and nothing has to be strapped together. **The larger correction is the framing.** §2.1a assumed the marker must be shared BETWEEN devices; it need not be. If each device is independently anchored to the common capture host — disciplined to 0.008 ppm on a box night — the device-to-device offset follows by subtraction. A cross-device marker is only required when there is no common host, i.e. the phone-capture case, not the box case; the co-location proposal solved a harder problem than the corpus poses. `0x03` is the stronger of the two for the axis problem and the opcode brief already said so: it converts "no timestamps at all" into host-stamped batches of known size with the residual bounded by one batch rather than accumulating across a night — which is precisely the drawn axis this work has been guarding against, removing the cause rather than refusing the symptom. Carries forward the opcode brief's own untested caveats rather than restating them as safe: whether buzzing mid-recording contaminates the ring's already-fragile motion column, the battery cost, and that `0x03` discards overflow past ~2 s with no error and no gap marker. Does NOT settle target 1 for the Polar pair, which has no commanded marker; for those, host anchoring suffices on box nights or §2.1a's co-location remains the route. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)
- Continues the consumer migration off `hostAxis.independent`. `tools/pat-host-offset.mjs` reads the inter-device offset from each device's {devMs, hostMs} pair and gated only on `independent` — which compares two COLUMNS and therefore reads MORE independent the coarser a synthesised counter's quantisation. Its PAT target is the Wellue finger PPG (line 371), and that is exactly the drawn-axis device: **O2Ring streams are flagged drawn 20/20** in the corpus, against **0/6 for H10 ecg** and **0/19 for Verity ppg**. Without the guard `toHostAxis` places beats on a fabricated timebase and the offset it yields is an artefact of the assumed rate. Now refuses on `deviceDrawn`, naming DRAWN in the refusal so a reader can tell it from an independence refusal. **ECGDex is deliberately NOT gated**: its exposure is zero measured (H10 ecg 0/6), and adding the guard there would force re-cutting a legitimate planted-drift fixture to defend against something that does not occur in the data — machinery for a hypothetical. Both refusals live in CLI loops rather than exported surfaces, so they are gated as source text; `tools/pat-host-offset.mjs` is now wired into BOTH scan lanes (the node `wanted` list and `Dex-Test-Suite.html` SOURCE_FILES), since a file in only one lane makes the scan read nothing in the other. **The new gate was vacuous on its first version and that is recorded rather than quietly fixed**: matching `/deviceDrawn === true/` also matched the refusal BODY (`ax.deviceDrawn === true ? …`), so replacing the whole guard with `if (false)` left it green. The regex now pins the conditional, verified by mutation — clean 4/4, mutant 2 failing. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

- Withdraws a second claim from the known-clock work: that the capture box is Stratum 2 and therefore **fails** its own `TIMEBASE_MAX_STRATUM = 1` rate-trust gate. Both halves are wrong. The box's own recorded decision reads `trust=disciplined absolute_ok=1 stratum=1 chrony_skew_ppm=0.004 timebase=host-disciplined` — it is host-disciplined and passes. The error: `chronyc tracking` reports `Stratum : 2` meaning THIS HOST's stratum, and `parse_chrony_tracking` normalises that to the SERVER's stratum by subtracting 1, so the value compared against the bound is 1. `host_clock.py`'s docstring warns about precisely this in capitals ("STRATUM MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AND THEY MUST NOT BE MIXED") and `test_chrony_stratum_is_normalised_to_the_SERVER_stratum` already gates it — there is no code defect and no missing test. The claim came from comparing a number read in a terminal against a constant read in a file, without ever running the function that connects them, and it was caught only by finally running the real path. Corrected in the parent brief (§2.1), the paper (Methods, now naming source-stratum 1 and the correction), the DOCS-INDEX row, and the follow-up brief — whose "re-derive `TIMEBASE_MAX_STRATUM` or document it" item is now **void**, since its premise was this error. What survives unchanged is the ratio that section actually rests on: the host's 19.5 us RMS offset is ~4 orders of magnitude tighter than the BLE jitter that immediately follows it, so PPS-disciplining buys precision the transport destroys. (`KNOWN-CLOCK-ADVERSARIAL-CAPTURE-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md`)

# Changelog

All notable changes to **Tepna — the Dex Suite** are recorded here. The format follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/), and the suite uses
[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) — one version number for the whole suite (the
"maintenance number"). See `docs/COMPLIANCE/SOFTWARE-LIFECYCLE-PLAN.md` for what MAJOR / MINOR /
PATCH mean against Tepna's published contracts, and `RELEASE-MANIFEST.json` for the machine-readable
ledger this file is the human view of.

> **How this file is maintained.** Do **not** hand-edit released sections. Each work-unit drops a
> collision-free changeset in `changes/` (see `changes/README.md`); `tools/release.mjs` folds all
> pending changesets into a new version section here, stamps `suite.manifest.json`, appends a
> `RELEASE-MANIFEST.json` record, and prunes `changes/`. The `release-ledger` gate keeps this file,
> the ledger, and the canonical version in agreement.

> ⚠️ **Reconstructed history.** Everything **below 1.0.0** (waves `0.1.0`–`0.9.0`) is *reconstructed
> from the DONE brief corpus* for provenance — those waves were **not** formally cut releases, and no
> per-app `manifestHash` snapshot is claimed for them. Formal, ledger-backed releases begin at
> **1.0.0**. Dates are the real brief execution dates.

## [2.5.0] — 2026-08-13

### Added
- Mutation-audit capture-host, and gate the diff on it.
- Close the mutation survivors in the three weakest capture-host modules, and bound the Python mutator so webmon can be measured at all.
- The Clock Contract is the least-tested module in the suite: **41 % of mutations to `clock.js` go unnoticed**. (`CLOCK-MUTATION-AUDIT-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`)
- Two gate holes found by `tools/mutate.mjs` on its first real sweep, now closed — and one triaged as not actionable. (`TOOL-INVOCABILITY-SWEEP-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`)
- `fitClockClosure` now refuses a source whose time axis carries no timing. Closure's claim is that three (`WEARABLE-HOST-AXIS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`)
- Assert the CPAPDex hero, oximetry, sessions and event-timeline builders — 19% → 36% of mutations killed. (`CLOCK-MUTATION-AUDIT-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`)
- Assert renderHistory and the clinical summary's band edges — 36% → 45% of mutations killed. (`CLOCK-AXIS-AND-RENDER-SURFACE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`)

- `_ckMk` validates the time components with `… || ms < 0 || ms > 999`, and a mutation of that last comparison to `ms >= 999` was the single mutant the full suite killed that the scoped `clock` selection missed — the entire accuracy cost of the group filter on this module, recorded in the brief's §1. It is now killed from inside the `clock` tag, so scoped and full agree for the first time. Three ISO fraction digits express exactly 0…999, so 999 is the largest value the grammar can produce; under the mutant `2026-08-05T23:15:42.999` — a real stamp, one millisecond before the second rolls — is refused as out of range. Adds `clock.js — wave 9: the millisecond band is closed at both ends` (5 assertions), written as the contract that a closed band contains both endpoints rather than as the shape of the mutant, and verified RED-under-mutant then GREEN-restored. Also corrects the brief's `✅ RESOLVED` figures: the `104/127 = 81.9 %` table predates #982, which found that 5 of those kills never ran and that generation itself was emitting 4 malformed mutants (`win >=> 1`) — so the numerator was inflated and the denominator four wide. Re-measured on two independent sweeps: 98/117 = 83.8 % full, 97/117 = 82.9 % scoped, with `invalid` deterministically 5 rather than the 1 assumed elsewhere. (`MUTATION-EQUIVALENCE-2026-08-04-BRIEF.md`)

- §3 asked for the `authorAliases` policy to live where a contributor meets it, and for the two entries whose alias came from the paper — because Crossref carries no author at all — to be distinguishable from the two that are Crossref-resolved. An alias suppresses a finding, so it is the one field in this ledger that can hide a real defect, and the two kinds are not the same evidential class: a `crossref-variant` alias is a spelling or short form of what Crossref recorded (`Du BOIS`/`DuBois`; the ESC/NASPE Task Force's full society name), so the check still rests on Crossref; a `from-paper` alias means Crossref has no author and the name was read off the paper being checked, which is mildly circular and is now marked rather than hidden. **Recording it was not enough** — this brief's sibling measured what happens to claims nobody re-checks, so `aliasSource` is required by the `citation-ledger` group and was watched failing three ways before being trusted: a missing marker, an unknown value, and `from-paper` asserted on a record where Crossref *did* record an author, which would launder a variant into the weaker class. The policy now sits in `CLAUDE.md` §📚 with the gate's scope (guides, `papers/**`, `docs/**.md`, root `*.js`; `briefs/` deliberately out at 35 % false positives; no DOI resolution, which needs network) and one prohibition worth naming: never silence a finding by editing `firstAuthor`, because the ledger is the oracle and no gate can catch that. (`CITATION-ATTRIBUTION-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-08-BRIEF.md`)
- `MUTATION-EQUIVALENCE` §6 left one box open — `clock.js`'s parse-family survivors — and nothing owned it: that brief scopes itself to the 15 in `hostAxis`, while `JS-DSP-MUTATION-FLEET` covers the DSP fleet and `clock.js` is the spine. Closed: **8 survivors, not the 14 the parent claims** (three waves plus wave 9 landed and nobody re-counted, on a byte-identical `clock.js`), and **0 killable** — radix-0 `parseInt` under a digits-only regex, an extra loop iteration that reads `undefined` and `continue`s, and over-determined `_ckMk` validation where disabling one redundant `!==` leaves the others to catch Feb 30. The verdicts match the earlier pass; what changed is that they are now **backed**. That pass ran a battery whose only positive control sat in `hostAxis`, which proves reach there and nothing about `parseTimestamp`/`_ckMk`/`resolveDMY`/`_ckDMY` — so "no distinguishing input" was a claim about the battery, not the code. Re-probed with in-family controls it came back **3 of 14 BLIND**, both causes being the battery's own: `_ckDMY(a, b, preferDMY, locked)` was called with one argument so the locked branch never executed, and `L94`'s `b > 12` needs `b` exactly 12. Fixed to 14/14 before any verdict was read. Earns a standing rule — **a positive control must live in the same function as the mutant it clears**, because an unreached mutant and an unkillable one produce identical output — and ships `tools/probe-clock-equivalence.mjs`, which enumerates mutants fresh, prints the control tally first, and marks the run BLIND if any control comes back equivalent. (`CLOCK-PARSE-EQUIVALENCE-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)
- A cpapdex battery — and the "least trustworthy number in the fleet map" turns out to be right, for a reason nobody had checked. (`MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)
- The crawl ranks survivors by **count**, and count is not value. On the first file whose work list was read seriously, CPAPDex's two largest killable clusters were **synthetic fixture generators** — `_synthEdfSet` (38 killable) and `_synthRaw` (11), the deterministic night that `regen-cpap-goldens.mjs` and the suite consume; `cohort-gen.js` even calls the first *"test-shaped"* in a comment. - Files the collision between two rules that are each individually right. §👥.1 mandates a worktree for bundle/ledger/DSP work; §🔏 says that same work owes a `verify-fixtures` re-run — and a worktree holds **134 of the 653** files in `uploads/`, because **435 are gitignored** and exist only in the primary checkout. The failure presents as *"the corpus is absent"*, which reads as a machine fact and is a checkout fact; measured this session, the corpus was one directory up the whole time. Also records the four places the corpus actually lives (including that `Ecg nightly` contains a space, and that the freshest 4,247 files are on `vigil` over ssh, so regenerating a genuinely-moved fixture may be an ssh job) and that a busy compute path can re-open its own verification debt between incurring and discharging — filed as a cost, not a hole, since CI reports it and `release.mjs` refuses on it. Proposal 1's mechanism is verified rather than sketched: `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir` resolves the primary checkout from inside a worktree, and degrades to `./uploads` elsewhere. (`FIXTURE-CORPUS-REACHABILITY-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)

- A motiondex battery — 32 classifications recorded, 23 real gaps named, and three families held back because their controls did not separate. (`MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)
- A mutation sweep produces **survivors**, and a survivor is not a work item. On `ppgdex-dsp.js`: 767 of them across 84 functions, roughly three quarters killable by no input at all. Turning that pile into work means, per survivor, finding an input that separates it from the original — mechanical, slow, and exactly what should run while nobody is watching. - `PATGate.verdict` refuses with `NO SHARED CLOCK` when handed a `hostAxis` reporting `independent === false`. Every quantity the gate weighs compares TWO DEVICES, so it presupposes they sit on one timebase; `independent === false` says the capture host's column was derived from the device stamp, so each device rides its own crystal and per-device wander lands directly in the beat-lag scatter, indistinguishable from the physiology the gate exists to measure. Measured 2026-08-09: all six H10 nights checked show a host↔device residual spread of 0.98 ms — one stamp quantum — against 101.89–5124 ms where a real second clock exists, so the entire raw corpus is phone-captured; the PAT verdict built on it attributed 84–99 ms of scatter to PTT variability, an attribution that is not identifiable without a shared clock. The axis argument is optional and LAST, so callers that pass nothing are byte-for-byte unchanged and only an explicit `false` refuses — the refusal names itself in `label` and carries the measured `spreadMs` in `why` so it can be audited rather than inferred. Verified red-by-value: 7 assertions fail without it. (`PAT-UNDER-PERBLOCK-ALIGNMENT-2026-08-02-BRIEF.md`)
- Add `tools/probe-equivalence.mjs` — a general equivalence prober with committed batteries, so a classification can be re-checked instead of believed. (`MUTATION-PROGRAM-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)
- Closes §2's concurrent-collision gap with a PR check, after the chosen mechanism turned out to be unavailable: GitHub merge queue is an organization-repository feature and `Tepna` is user-owned, so the API rejects a `merge_queue` rule outright — verified 2026-08-09, and the rejection is schema-level, identical with no parameters at all. The check asks whether this PR edits a shared doc that already moved on the base branch since the branch point. That case produces **no git conflict** — the constructed scenario auto-merges cleanly — which is exactly why nothing else catches it and why it happened twice on one file in one day. Scope is deliberately the same guarded set as `guard-stale-brief.sh` (`briefs/*.md` + `DOCS-INDEX.md`): gating source would be red on nearly every PR at this merge rate, burying the finding, and source is the case git already handles by conflicting. Unlike the local hook it reads the real ref rather than your last fetch, and it does not care whether the bytes arrived via `Edit`, `Write` or a `Bash` heredoc — the two holes that let this through before. Verified on three constructed cases before being trusted: it fires on the collision, stays quiet on source-only churn, and stays quiet once the branch is rebased. The first run of case B reported a false failure and the cause was the **test**, not the check — recorded in the test file, because a verifier wrong in the passing direction is the failure this check exists to catch. Ships the scenario as `stale-file.test.sh` so the logic can be re-checked rather than trusted once. (`BRIEF-COLLISION-RESIDUAL-GAP-2026-08-09-BRIEF.md`)
